市場焦點
美國
• 10月S&P Global綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖較前月回落，但仍處高檔區間；服務業動
能略為放緩，製造業PMI則升至56.0，創近4年半新高。新訂單增速加快 ，顯示企業需求、投
資及庫存回補動能仍具韌性 ，然而企業售價與投入成本同步上升 ，反映需求改善 、供應鏈延
遲及成本轉嫁效應持續存在 ，通膨降溫進程仍具不確定性 。整體而言 ，市場關注焦點轉向通
膨與利率走勢，高利率環境恐維持較長時間，進而推高股債資產評價波動。
中國
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季 GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期 ，在出口與科技製造業帶動下 ，
短期成長可望維持穩定 ，但若內需未能有效改善 ，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散 ，建議聚焦在
半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈 ，及高端製造相關產業 ，至於消費 、房地產及傳統內需類股 ，

仍需觀察後續刺激政策與消費信心回升情況。
法國
政局動盪
• 法國金融市場因各地抗議活動持續擴大 ，面臨股債雙跌的局面。市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支
出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮 。法國10年期公債殖利率一度突破 5%，法德利差亦擴
大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標仍處於歷史相對低檔 ，遠低於歐
元區主權債務危機期間，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務危機的風險仍相對有限。

美國PMI維持強勁擴張，經濟成長續航但通膨壓力再升溫
• 企業需求強勁支撐美國景氣維持高檔擴張 ：美國10月綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖略低於前月，但仍處於近五年來
的高位區間，顯示企業活動持續高速擴張；其中服務業 PMI為57.9、製造業PMI升至56.0並創53個月新高，新訂單增速
更創逾4年半最佳表現，反映內需穩健、出口需求改善與企業補庫存效應同步發酵，美國第四季經濟動能仍具韌性。
• 成本上升與供應瓶頸推升通膨風險： 企業售價漲幅創2022年8月以來最高，能源、運輸、金屬原料及薪資成本同步上揚 ，
加上供應鏈交期延長與訂單積壓擴大，使企業得以持續轉嫁成本，暗示未來數月消費者通膨可能再度偏離聯準會目標區
間，市場對利率維持較高水準的預期恐因此升溫。
• 後市觀察：景氣韌性有利獲利成長，但通膨可能推升殖利率、壓抑高評價資產。投資配置建議優先布局美國大型科技股 、

美國金融股及工業資本支出受惠族群，可透過NASDAQ 100 ETF、S&P 500 ETF及美國銀行股ETF參與企業獲利成長；
固定收益則以短天期投資級債為主，以降低利率波動風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.24

海外資金流入美國市場，支撐美國資產吸引力
• 海外資金持續流入美國：美國財政部8月TIC資料顯示，
整體資金淨流入 1,998億美元，其中民間部門淨流入
1,785億美元，為主要資金來源；外資淨買進美國長
期證券3,007億美元，遠高於美國投資人加碼海外資
產216億美元，反映全球資金在經濟成長與流動性考
量下，對美國金融市場仍維持高度的配置需求。
• 全球資金偏好美國資產： 外資 8月淨買入美國股票
1,319億美元，高於美國公債與公司債 ，顯示海外投
資人仍看好美國企業獲利與經濟基本面 ，雖然短期國
庫券持有量下降，部分跨境美元資金亦出現波動，但
以10月近況來看長天期公債標售仍吸引境外央行 、
保險資金及大型資產管理機構參與，反映高殖利率水
準對長期資金仍具吸引力。在經濟成長維持韌性與企

業獲利支撐下，美國資產仍為全球資金配置的重要核
心市場。
• 市場看法：海外資金流入有助反映美國資產吸引力，
惟後續仍須觀察通膨 、Fed政策及企業獲利表現 。投
資布局建議可以美國投資等級債、美國中短天期公債
及大型AI基礎建設受惠族群為核心配置 ，兼顧收益、
防禦及長期成長潛力。

顧
歷史數據顯示全球股市突破歷史新高後，後續上漲機率高
• 很多人直覺以為見高就是頂，但過去20年的統計資料顯示，全球股市在創下新高後，多數時間選擇繼續向上走。
• 勝率顯著偏高： 全球股市 (MSCI全球指數 )創新高後持有一年 ，獲得正報酬的機率高達 62%，三個月後的正報酬機率更達
71%。美股與台股表現最亮眼，標普500指數創高後一年上漲機率為76%，那斯達克為79%；而台灣股市表現更為驚人，創
高後三個月內上漲的機率高達82%。
• 下檔拉回風險有限：在創高後短期(一個月內)修正幅度其實相當溫和 ，全球多數主要市場的回徹幅度中位數僅 1%～2%左右。
• 突破前高優於連續創新高： 若市場在歷經至少一個月以上的整理後突破前高 ，其未來一年的平均報酬率高達 8.4%，遠高於

連續每天創新高的3.9%。這代表市場經適度整理後的突破，具備更健康的上漲動能。
創新高後的平均遠期報酬率
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22

顧
市場寬度收窄，由少數巨頭獨撐
• 當前的市場特徵由少數巨頭帶頭衝： 科技股權重較高的指數(如那斯達克、台股、韓股)，長期以來都有少數權重股拉動指數
的現象。自2024年以來，全球13個主要市場中有10個市場在創新高時，參與上漲的股票比例明顯下降 。已開發市場創新高
時高於200日均線的股票比例從過去 75.5%降至65.1%，新興市場更降至 61.3%。這意味著指數看似風光 ，但多數個股並未
同步創高，當漲勢過度集中於少數大型科技巨頭時，整體市場的抗風險能力會相對減弱。
• 小結與投資建議：歷史經驗顯示，創新高本身是強勢市場的特徵，後續繼續走揚的機率遠大於立刻崩盤。然而，鑑於目前市
場寬度收窄、權重高度集中於科技股的現狀 ，盲目追高個股的風險已顯著增加 。投資建議可以利用ETF掌握大盤趨勢，由於

指數上漲主要由龍頭股驅動 ，透過市值型ETF(如追蹤標普500ETF)能直接享受龍頭巨頭帶來的指數創高紅利 ，避免選錯個股
而賺了指數、賠了價差。此外，提防短期股市回檔，可運用定期定額分批布局或等待大盤適度拉回整理再行加碼，降低追高
風險。
創新高時不同水準的市場寬度佔比
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.10.22
已開發市場和新興市場創新高時的寬度

法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓
• 法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓：法國各地抗議活動持續擴大，最初由學生發起，隨後因生活成本高漲而引發其
他群體響應。在財政空間有限、俄烏戰爭與美伊衝突推升能源價格的背景下，政府難以有效緩解民生成本壓力，法國金融市
場因此面臨股債雙跌的局面。
• 公債殖利率攀升，但系統性風險仍相對可控：市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10
年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標（CISS）仍處於
歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐元區主權債務危機期間，顯示金融市場尚未出現廣泛的系統性壓力，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務
危機的風險仍相對有限。
G7社會動盪指數，法國高居榜首

資料來源： Beco models country，2026.10.19 資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.19

資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.15 資料來源： tradingeconomics，2026.10.21
日本貿易收支
• 日本貿易逆差擴大，進口增幅高於出口：日本2026年9月貿易逆差擴大至5,366億日圓，較去年同期的2,777億日圓增加93%，
高於市場預期的1,810億日圓，連續第五個月呈現貿易赤字。出口年增16.7%至10.97兆日圓，受惠於半導體相關電子零件、
製造設備及汽車出口成長；惟進口年增18.9%至11.51兆日圓，增幅高於出口，主要受到原油、半導體電子零件及液化天然
氣進口增加帶動。其中，原油進口年增51.1%，部分反映日本在荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張下，積極分散能源採購來源。
• 日本央行本週料維持利率不變：日本央行9月將政策利率調升25個基點至1.25%，創31年來新高。考量前幾次升息對經濟的

傳導效果仍待觀察，加上貿易逆差擴大反映進口成本壓力，多數分析師預期日本央行本週將暫停升息，以評估貨幣緊縮對經
濟與通膨的影響。

中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期：顯示中國經濟在第二季落底後呈現溫和回升 ，從季度表現來看，
一、二、三季增速分別為5.0%、4.3%及4.5%，反映政策支持與外需復甦開始發揮效果 ，全年達成官方4.5%成長目標的機率
明顯提升，第三季經濟改善的主要動能來自出口 ，受惠於全球企業持續擴建 AI基礎設施，及科技產品需求強勁 ，中國高科技
產品出口表現亮眼 ，尤其是積體電路 、伺服器、電腦零組件，及儲存設備等產品增幅顯著 ，高技術製造業維持雙位數成長 。
同時，中國對美國及歐盟出口維持強勁 ，推升貿易順差 走升，為支撐經濟的重要力量 。然而，內需疲弱仍是當前經濟最大隱
憂，房地產調整尚未結束，居民消費與企業投資意願保守，使經濟復甦結構呈現「外熱內冷」的特徵。

• 展望後市：在出口與科技製造業帶動下 ，短期成長可望維持穩定 ，但若內需未能有效改善 ，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散 ，建
議聚焦在半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈 ，及高端製造相關產業 ，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股 ，仍需觀察後續刺激政
策與消費信心回升情況。
資料來源：開源證券，2026.10.22

彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高
• 彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高：彭博產業研究認為，中國AI產業未來將由大型科技平台主導 ，其中騰
訊、字節跳動與阿里巴巴憑藉龐大用戶基礎 、數據資源、資金實力及完整生態系統 ，在AI競賽中具備明顯優勢 ，這些企業可將
AI廣泛應用於電商、社群、遊戲、金融與雲端業務，提升營運效率並強化競爭門檻，例如：阿里巴巴利用 AI 驅動電商平台上的
個人化產品推薦，而騰訊則運用 AI 優化短影音廣告並強化微信功能。騰訊自 2026 年 7 月起開始將混元 AI 模型整合至其電子
遊戲中，使遊戲角色及助理能更準確地回應玩家 ，然而，AI模型訓練與基礎建設投入龐大 ，獲利能力短期仍受壓抑 ，且須面對
監管、資料隱私及晶片供應等挑戰 。此外，智譜、月之暗面、MiniMax及商湯等AI新創雖具技術創新能力，但缺乏規模效益、

穩定現金流及長期資本支持，未來獲利挑戰較大。
• 投資建議：看好中國AI長期發展者，可優先關注具資金 、算力與應用場景優勢的大型平台企業 ，受惠程度可望高於多數 AI新創
公司。

訂單將持續亮麗，估落在1,125億至1,159億美元，有望連三個月破千億美元。

布前，市場觀望AI投資是否存在泡沫化風險 。短期來看，台股在季線附近出現支撐 ，不過在11月初的美國期中選舉落幕前 ，
台股維持區間震盪的可能性較高；中期來看，美國期中選後往往因政治不確定性下降，歷史統計股市上漲機率高，加上台灣

年底集團作帳及選舉行情，正向看待年底台股表現。

2026年 10月 30日 全7頁

2026年 7-9月期 GDP（1次速報）予測 
～前期比年率＋1.5％を予想～ 
個人消費は小幅減だった一方、設備投資と輸出が実質GDPを押し上げ

経済調査部 エコノミスト 畑中 宏仁 
  チーフエコノミスト 神田 慶司

[要約] 
◼ 11月 16日公表予定の 2026年 7-9月期の GDP1次速報では、実質 GDPが前期比年率＋
1.5％（前期比＋0.4％）と、4四半期連続のプラス成長を予想する。個人消費は小幅
に減少したが、設備投資と輸出の増加が実質GDPを押し上げたようだ。 
◼ 個人消費はサービスが増加したものの、財が減少した影響の方が大きかったと予想す
る。設備投資は 4-6 月期に特殊要因で減少していた反動もあって増加したとみられる。
公共投資は減少、政府消費は増加したと見込んでいる。 
◼ 輸出と輸入はサービスが減少した一方、財が増加したと予想する。代替調達の進展に
よる鉱物性燃料輸入増加は実質 GDPを押し下げたが、石油国家備蓄の大規模放出が行
われなかったことで公的在庫変動が実質 GDP成長率を押し上げた分が相殺したようだ。

### Page 2

1

2 / 7

1．2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋1.5％を予想 
11月 16日公表予定の 2026年 7-9月期の GDP1次速報では、実質 GDPが前期比年率＋1.5％
（前期比＋0.4％）と、4四半期連続のプラス成長を予想する（10月30日正午までに公表された
基礎統計に基づく）。個人消費は小幅に減少したが、設備投資と輸出が実質GDPを押し上げたよ
うだ。 
個人消費はサービスが増加したものの、財が減少した影響の方が大きかったと予想する。設
備投資は4-6月期に特殊要因で減少していた反動もあって増加したとみられる。 
輸出と輸入はサービスが減少したものの、財が増加したと予想する。中東産原油の代替調達
の進展による鉱物性燃料輸入の増加は実質 GDPを押し下げたが、石油国家備蓄の大規模放出が
行われなかったことで公的在庫変動が実質GDP成長率を押し上げた分で相殺されたようだ。

図表 2：実質 GDP成長率と需要項目別寄与度の推移

民需：個人消費、住宅投資は減少の一方、設備投資は大幅な増加を予想 
個人消費は前期比▲0.1％と予想する。4-6月期のインバウンド（個人消費の控除項目）の実
績が上方改定されるであろうことを踏まえると、2 四半期連続のマイナスとなると見込んでい
る。サービスは増加したものの、耐久財と半耐久財、非耐久財がそろって減少したとみている。
耐久財では、自動車税の環境性能割廃止やエアコンの省エネ基準の引き上げの影響もあって 4-
6 月期に増加していた自動車や家電への支出が減少したようだ。半耐久財・非耐久財では衣服
やたばこ、ガソリンなどが増加したものの、食料品などが減少したとみられる。サービスは外
食や鉄道旅客、娯楽などが増加したようだ。 
住宅投資は前期比▲1.8％と予想する。国土交通省によると、7月の建設工事費デフレーター
（住宅総合、大和総研による季節調整値）が前月比＋6.8％と高い伸びを示す中にあっても、7、
8 月平均の新設住宅着工戸数（国土交通省による季節調整値）は、分譲住宅の増加に支えられ
-1.0

### Page 3

1

3 / 7

4-6月平均比＋1.0％となった（巻末の関連指標②上段右）。しかし、進捗ベースで計上される
GDP統計上は、建設資材等の価格上昇の影響により実質では減少したとみられる。 
設備投資は前期比＋2.0％と予想する。建設投資は減少したものの、機械投資と知的財産生
産物投資（ソフトウェア、研究開発など）が増加したようだ。研究開発投資の増加には、4-6
月期に特殊要因で研究開発サービスの受取（設備投資の控除項目）が大幅に増加したことの反
動も含まれる。経済産業省によると、資本財総供給指数は、国産品と輸入品が共に増加し、7-
9 月期は前期比＋4.9％となった（巻末の関連指標②中段左）。一方、建設投資は、国土交通省
によると、民間非居住用建築・土木の出来高（名目額、大和総研による季節調整値）の7、8月
平均は 4-6月平均比＋1.2％となったが、7月の建設工事費デフレーター（非住宅総合、大和総
研による季節調整値）は前月比＋7.3％と大きく上昇しており、実質では減少したとみられる。 
民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.0％ptと予想する。仮置き値の原材料在庫は同＋0.1％pt、
仕掛品在庫は同▲0.1％pt となった。その他では製品在庫が同＋ 0.1％pt、流通在庫が同▲
0.1％ptだったとみられる。

公需：公共投資は減少、政府消費は増加と予想 
公共投資は前期比▲1.0％と予想する。国土交通省によると、7、8 月平均の公共工事出来高
（名目額、大和総研による季節調整値）は 4-6月平均比▲2.2％と減少傾向にある（巻末の関連
指標②中段右）。25年度補正予算に盛り込まれた公共事業の執行が一服したとみられる。加え
て、工事費の上昇も重しとなっている。 
政府消費は前期比＋0.2％と予想する。退職金支払いの増加や私立高校授業料の実質無償化、
公立小学校の給食費無償化もあって高い伸びとなった 4-6月期に続き、医療費の増加により 7-
9月期の政府消費も増加したとみられる。 
公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.3％ptと予想する。4-6月期と異なり、7-9月期には大規模
な石油の国家備蓄放出は行われず、公的在庫変動は実質GDP成長率を押し上げたとみられる。

外需：輸入増が輸出増を上回り、外需寄与度は▲0.2％ptを予想 
輸出は前期比＋0.5％と予想する。サービスは減少したものの、財は増加したと見込んでい
る。財では半導体等製造装置や化学製品、鉱物性燃料などが増加したようだ。サービスでは、
4-6月期に大幅に増加していた研究開発サービスの反動減に加え、産業財産権等使用料やイン
バウンドなども減少したとみている。 
輸入は前期比＋1.8％と予想する。輸出と同様、サービスは減少したものの、財は増加した
と見込んでいる。財では、代替調達の進展により鉱物性燃料が大きく増加したほか、電算機類
も増加したようだ。サービスではアウトバウンドは増加したものの、研究開発サービスや著作
権等使用料などが減少したとみられる。

### Page 4

1

4 / 7

輸入増が輸出増を上回り、純輸出（外需）の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度は前期比▲0.2％
ptになったと予想する。

2．10-12月期の実質 GDPはおおむね横ばいを予想 
2026年 10-12月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比でおおむね横ばいになると見込んでいる。個人
消費は小幅に増加、設備投資は増加すると予想する。輸出はAI関連需要を背景とした米国向け
輸出を中心に増加するものの、輸入の伸びが上回り、外需はマイナスになるとみている。 
個人消費は小幅に増加すると予想する。厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計調査」の現金給与総額は
前年同月比で 3％を超える伸びが続いていることに加え、10月以降は各地で最低賃金が引き上
げられることから、名目賃金の高い伸びは続くだろう。一方、9月の消費者物価指数（除く生
鮮食品）は前年同月比＋2.1％と、8月から 0.4％pt伸び幅が拡大した。政策要因による影響を
差し引いたとしても、食料品等を中心に物価上昇率は高まっており、実質賃金上昇率を押し下
げるだろう。エネルギー価格についても、電気・ガス料金補助は12月使用分（1月請求分）か
ら再開されるものの、現行の補助は 9月使用分（10月請求分）で一旦終了するため、10-12月
期の物価は押し上げられる。加えて、ガソリン補助金の基準価格が12月以降引き上げられるこ
とや、燃料費・原料費調整制度によって資源価格高騰の影響がラグを持って電気・ガス料金に
反映されることも物価の押し上げ要因となろう1。 
設備投資は増加すると予想する。日本銀行「全国企業短期経済観測調査」 （日銀短観）によ
ると、9月調査時点での 2026年度の設備投資計画（全規模全産業、除く土地、含むソフトウェ
ア・研究開発）は前年度比＋9.3％と、前年の 9月調査時点（同＋9.5％）と同程度の伸びが示
された。建設資材価格と労務費の上昇が建設投資を下押しするものの、省力化投資やAI関連投
資といった設備投資の増加基調は続くと見込む。 
住宅投資は建設資材と労務費上昇の影響を受けて減少すると見込んでいる。公共投資は工事
費の高騰を背景に横ばい圏で推移すると予想する。政府消費は高齢化などに伴う医療費・介護
費増により増加すると見込んでいる。 
輸出は増加すると予想する。米国の設備投資は中東情勢をめぐる不透明感や金利上昇の中に
あっても、AI関連需要を背景に増加が続くだろう。米国内での設備投資の増加は米国向け輸出
の追い風となり、財輸出は増加すると見込んでいる。一方、インバウンドは中国政府による日
本への渡航自粛要請が続く中、小幅な増加にとどまるだろう。 
輸入は増加すると予想する。経済産業省によると、9月の原油調達について、バブ・エル・
マンデブ海峡経由で調達予定であった原油の輸送ルートをスエズ運河経由に切り替えたことで

1 12月以降の電気・ガス料金補助とガソリン補助金に関する政府の対応については、首相官邸「中東情勢を踏
まえた『緊急的激変緩和措置』の今後の対応についての会見」（2026年10月 29日）を参照。

### Page 5

1

5 / 7

到着が遅れた分は備蓄原油の既放出分で対応したという2。10月は前年平月比 10割の調達が実
現できる見通しとのことであり、11月以降も同量を確保できるのであれば、財輸入は押し上げ
られるだろう。 
リスク要因は中東情勢の悪化とAI関連需要の下振れである。中東情勢が悪化し、原油価格が
さらに高騰すれば、物価高を通じて個人消費を一段と下押しするだろう。また、足元の米国経
済では設備投資が景気を下支えしており、とりわけデータセンターや半導体などのIT関連投資
がけん引役となっている。10月半ばにかけて、一部AI関連企業の財務上の脆弱性が意識され、
株価が下落する局面があったが、こうした懸念がAI関連企業全体に広がれば、外部資金への依
存度が高い企業を中心に設備投資が鈍化し得る3。その場合、米国向け輸出が下押しされる可能
性があり、注意が必要だ。

2 詳細は、第 5回中東情勢に伴う重要物資の安定的な供給確保のためのタスクフォース（2026年 10月2日）
における経済産業省提出資料を参照。 
3 詳細は、矢作大祐・藤原翼「米国経済見通し CoreWeave問題をどうみるか」（大和総研レポート、2026年
10月22日）を参照。

鴻海 (2317 TT) 
上修獲利效益預期

永豐觀點 
垂直整合能力，維持 AI 伺服器領先地位，營收規模擴大，上修獲利效益預期。 
 投資評價與建議 
研究部維持鴻海買進建議：鴻海集團於科技日展現垂直整合、精密加工能力，並憑
藉著規模化交付能力， 預期於伺服器市場維持四成以上市占率 、並能優先取得新世
代產品參考設計， 營收規模放大的同時體現獲利率效益， 獲利金額擴大以及營運資
金運用，並樹立組裝產業進入門檻。研究部參考近期公告之財務及產業資訊，預期
鴻海 2H26~2027 年營運具上修空間 ， 調整後預估鴻海 2026 年/2027 年稅後 EPS 
18.79 元/25.46 元。惟近期總體利率環境影響股價評價，目標本益比從 15 倍調整
至 14 倍，維持鴻海買進建議，目標價 356 元(14x2027FEPS)。

ESG 評析 
鴻海企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 A 等與 A 等。

### Page 2

2

鴻海為專業 ODM/EMS 廠商，產品涵蓋手機、伺服器、平板、網通設備等終端產
品，以及各類電子零組件如連接器、連接線等，旗下重要子公司包含工業富聯
(601138 SH)、富智康(2038 HK)及鴻騰精密(6088 HK)，近年公司開始積極投入研
發資源在電動車 、 數位醫療 、 機器人 、AI、 半導體 、5G 等領域 。 主要客戶為Apple、
HP、Dell、Sony、Cisco、北美四大 CSP 等。2Q26 產品營收占比：消費暨智能
29%、雲端網路 51%、電腦終端 15%、元件及其他 5%。 
鴻海科技日展現垂直整合能力  統合零組件到機櫃生產：(1) 鴻海科技日強調展現
垂直整合能力及精密加工能力，提供客戶 one stop shop，垂直整合、設計製造服
務。鴻海具備伺服器 Level 1 到 Level 12 的生產能力，從螺絲、零組件、機構件
等至伺服器系統整機櫃。鴻海團隊從產品設計開始即整合組裝、良率、服務零組件
到整系統最佳化的解決方案。(2) 鴻海協助輝達、Google 打造 AI 工廠、AI 基礎
建設，以自動化縮短資料中心建造時程。(3) 施耐德電機、東元提供電力設備，與
鴻海進一步合作打造模組化資料中心。 4. 本田與三菱電機展示與鴻海在電動車未
來自動駕駛、駕駛監控系統、 ECU 等合作方向。 (5) 鴻海同時推出新電動車平
台,Model A+ 、K 系列。A+為休旅車，其特色為取消 B 柱讓車內寬敞，並將配置 
L4 自動駕駛感測器。Model K 為輕型卡車，提供短程 120 公里、長程 250 公里車
型選擇。 
展場上鴻海集團展出 AI 伺服器相關產品，1. 與 Vertiv 合作液冷基建的 AI Data 
Hall 解決方案。(2) 展出 NVIDIA DSX POD 解決方案，以及 Vera Rubin 世代 
Compute Tray、Switch Tray、CPO Switch、CPU Tray、LPU Tray 等相關產品。
(3) Google 下一代 TPU 將使用的 OCS 全光交換機。(4) 在 NVIDIA CPO Switch
使用 的精 密 光學 對位 機 構 件、FAU 光纖 連 接產 品。(5) 集團 公司 鴻 騰精密
(6088.HK)， 展出液冷相關零組件QD、Manifold； 電力相關零組件Busbar、Power 
Whip；主板上連接器 Paladin、SOCAMM 等。甚至下一代 Vera Rubin Ultra 將
採用 ZQD、POGO 連接器也已取得 NPI。 
美系大廠資本支出 2027 年上修至 6 成以上增長 建置需求未歇：上週美股法說會
後，統計 Bloomberg 美系五大 CSP 加上 Space X，2026 年資本支出從年增率從
分別從 102%上修至 103%，2027 年從 44%上修至 60%，2026 年及 2027 年資
本支出金額分別達 8,793 億美元及 1.41 兆美元，顯示AI 算力建置需求仍未停歇。 
上修 3Q26、4Q26 營收獲利預估：鴻海先前公告 3Q26 營收達 3.0 兆優於預期，
以及子公司工業富聯公告 3Q26 財報優於預期。以此推算鴻海營收擴大規模效益
帶來獲利表現上修，研究部預期營益率增長至 3.8%，稅後 EPS 上修至 5.10 元。
上修 4Q26 營收預期，主因3Q26 營運基期墊高，以及iPhone 維持出貨動能、GB
機櫃出貨量出貨量、Vera Rubin 機櫃單價跳升等，推算帶動營收季增 14%，稅後
EPS 上修至 5.85 元。

營運現況與分析

### Page 3

3

AI 伺服器需求為鴻海營運增長動能：AI 伺服器需求主要由美系 CSP、AI 模型商及
Neocloud 驅動，鴻海 GPU AI 伺服器除了原本美系 CSP 需求，也將有新型雲端
客戶開始貢獻。研究部預期鴻海維持 VR200 的主要供應商角色，整體市占率維持
達 40%以上，近期上修預估整體市場 Vera Rubin 至約 64,000 櫃 NVL72 (市場預
期為 80,000~90,000 櫃)，同時鴻海 2027 年將增加美系 CSP AI ASIC 伺服器機櫃
供應，為 2027 年營運增長動能，鴻海在提高市占率、營收規模放大的同時體現獲
利率效益、獲利金額擴大以及營運資金運用，並能樹立組裝產業進入門檻。 
綜合以上，上修後預估 2026 年營收 11.1 兆，年增 37%，毛利率 6.0%，營益率
3.7%，稅後淨利 2,627 億，年增 39%，稅後 EPS 18.79 元。2027 年營收 15.5 兆
元，年增 39%，毛利率 5.4%，營益率 3.4%，稅後淨利 3,555 億元，年增 35%，
稅後 EPS 25.46 元。

### Page 1

M
 Update
UMC  | Asia Pacific
  Near term in line but larger 
long-term AI opportunity; stay 
OW
Larger AI opportunity into 2029: The company raised 2029 AI-related revenue 
guidance from >US$1bn to 25% of total revenue. Assuming ~15% YoY growth on our 
2028e revenue (c. US$14bn), 2029 pro forma AI revenue could reach ~US$4bn (25% 
of ~US$16bn) –  well above the prior US$1bn. Also, newly announced 12-inch silicon 
photonics capacity of 18kwpm could generate at least US$1.7–1.8bn assuming ~US
$8k wafer ASP – not to mention newly announced capacity of another 22kwpm for 
DTC/silicon capacitors and 20kwpm for Teflon modulators.
2027 price hike plan in line with our estimate: The company guided for a 10% 
improvement in 2027 blended wafer ASP. Excluding better product mix, like-for-like 
wafer ASP would rise 5–10%, which is in line with our estimate but slightly below 
the buy-side bogey of 10-15%. 
Weak consumer could be covered by other non-AI demand: UMC does  see some 
weakness showing up in PCs and midrange/low-end smartphones, but 
communications,  automotive, and industrial demand remains robust.

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.

universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

Taiwan Semis: GUC (3443.TW): Mgmt. Call takeaways: Server CPU ramp 
to sustain momentum into 2027; opportunities across applications
31 October 2026 | 12:35AM HKT
We talked to GUC (3443.TW, not covered) management recently. The company’s Sep 
revenues were up 22% MoM (vs. +1% MoM in Aug), reflecting its revenues from CPU 
of a leading US CSP in strong ramp up with more foundry capacity rolling out, and 
would continue to support the growth in 4Q26 and 2027 per the company. The 
positive tone echoes our positive view on Global Servers TAM, which we expect 
leading US cloud service providers (CSPs) in aggregate will grow their capex budgets 
by 84%/ 52%/ 13% YoY in 2026-28E, with our estimates being raised by 4% / 17% / 
22% in 2026-28E to US$1,397bn by 2028E. Revenues in 3Q26 are driven by both 
NRE and turnkey solutions, with turnkey solutions revenues up 44% QoQ on the CPU 
ramp up, and despite turnkey solutions up to ~90% of 3Q26 revenues, the GM 
sustained at 20%+ in 3Q26, thanks to higher-margin NRE milestones. GUC adjusts 
pricing to reflect rising wafer and memory costs, and management sees memory 
impact is manageable on its customers’ projects. On competition, management 
highlighted its position as a core partner of TSMC, with in-house IP for 3D stacking as 
a key differentiator. GUC’s strong turnkey pull-in from its CSP customer’s server CPU 
and its focus on IP for TSMC 3D stacking point to continued demand for TSMC’s 
(2330.TW, Buy) advanced nodes and packaging. The positive tone on ASIC demand 
also echoes our positive view on rising ASIC penetration rate (~60% by 2028E, report 
link), supported by leading CSPs diversifying thier AI chips platforms and reducing 
token per second per costs, driving inferencing / training efficiency.

Company profile: GUC (3443.TW, not covered) is a ASIC design service company 
and a TSMC affiliate, providing NRE design services and turnkey production for

### Page 2

custom chips manufactured at TSMC. Its customers span cloud (CPUs, AI accelerators,
BMCs), automotive and consumer (image sensors, SSD controllers), with 68% of its
products under 7nm.
Key takeaways
1. Mass production as the key profit driver: Management noted that the key to
profitability lies in converting NRE projects into mass production. With the largest
customer’s CPU now in volume production, turnkey has become the main growth driver.
NRE carries higher GM than turnkey, but as it is recognized by milestone, quarterly NRE

margin varies with the mix of projects at different stages.
2. Expanding opportunities across cloud and automotive: GUC continues to open up
new opportunities across applications. In cloud, it works with all major US CSPs on
projects spanning CPUs and GPUs and also supplies other server projects such as BMCs,
while actively pursuing further AI accelerator opportunities. In automotive, it is engaged
with both US and Chinese customers at the NRE stage, two of which already plan for

mass production.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

研 究  報 告                
十銓(4967.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
深化 B2B 布局開啟成⻑動能 
 投資評價與建議 
2026年營運受惠記憶體價格上漲及產品組合優化，2027年隨著新廠投產、企
業級客戶導入及邊緣AI需求；若公司能維持穩定貨源並有效控制庫存成本，獲
利有望維持高檔。 
 重點評論分析 
受惠記憶體價格漲勢持續 ， 推升營收獲利 ：3Q26營收季增33.6%至101.4億元 ，
年增143.5%，受惠記憶體報價上漲、產品組合優化帶動毛利率上升至49.3%，
營業利益率44.5%，本期淨利36.18億元，稅後EPS 37.19元，優於預期。 
庫存調配、產品售價同步拉升，有望支撐獲利：終端市況需求呈現分化，其中，  
AI資料中心跟企業伺服器、邊緣運算等較高階需求強，約佔整體DRAM產出
70% ；另消費端換機需求受到零組件成本上漲壓抑整體需求， 惟高階電競超
頻與AI創作者在快速運算跟大容量需求下仍強。在原廠優先生產資源向HBM、
高階DDR5傾斜下，除了減少DDR4亦排擠其他應用市場產能，將使工控、網
通、⾞用等⻑週期性客戶，⾯臨缺料與成本上升壓⼒。尤其部分工控及網通應
用仍依賴DDR4。截3Q26季底庫存達172億元，其中，DRAM約佔75%、Flash
約25%，公司現⾏庫存策略係以伺服器、工控⻑單及高階B2C為主。時序進入
4Q26，公司雖並未給予財測，但基於庫存調配及產品結構持續優化下，推估
4Q26營收107.９億元，毛利率52.3%，稅後EPS 48.6元。 
 投資風險 
記憶體價格反轉與庫存跌價風險、終端需求疲軟

### Page 2

2 
| 營運概況 
受惠記憶體價格漲勢持續，推升營收獲利：3Q26 營收季增 33.6%至 101.4 億元，年增 143.5%，受惠記憶體報價
上漲、產品組合優化帶動毛利率上升至 49.3%，營業利益率 44.5%，本期淨利 36.18 億元，稅後 EPS 37.19 元，
優於預期。市場別部份，至前三季 B2B(企業暨工控應用)營收從去年同期的 24.6 上升至 64.6%，B2C(品牌暨通路)
則降至 35.40%。 
庫存調配、產品售價同步拉升，有望支撐獲利：終端市況需求呈現分化，其中，AI 資料中心跟企業伺服器、邊緣
運算等較高階需求強，約佔整體 DRAM 產出 70%；另消費端換機需求受到零組件成本上漲壓抑整體需求，惟高
階電競超頻與 AI 創作者在快速運算跟大容量需求下仍強。在原廠優先生產資源向 HBM、高階 DDR5 傾斜下，除
了減少 DDR4 亦排擠其他應用市場產能，將使工控、網通、⾞用等⻑週期性客戶，⾯臨缺料與成本上升壓⼒。尤
其部分工控及網通應用仍依賴 DDR4。截 3Q26 季底庫存達 172 億元，其中，DRAM 約佔 75%、Flash 約 25%，
在庫存量部份，DDR4 占比約 60.09%，DDR5 約 38.51%，主要較早布局 DDR4 庫存；公司現⾏庫存策略係以伺
服器 、 工控⻑單及高階 B2C 為主 。 進入 4Q26 ， 公司並未給予財測 ， 但基於庫存調配及產品結構優化下 ， 推估 4Q26
營收 107.9 億元，毛利率 52.3%，稅後 EPS 48.6 元。 
2027 年前無價格下行趨勢：展望 2027 年，管理階層表示除了三大原廠之外，包含中國系廠記憶體廠，目前產量
以及未來 1~3 年陸續新增的產量，預期整體供需平衡至少要至 2028 年下半年或以後，且 4Q26 至 2Q27 係為缺
貨最嚴峻時期，2027 年價格難以見到反轉。產能規劃方⾯，新北約千坪自動化新廠預計 1Q27 進⾏裝修及設備進
駐 ， 2Q27 完工投產 ， 整體產能預估增加約 50% 。 十銓將事業群分為企業暨工控應用(B2B) ， 以及品牌暨通路(B2C) ， 
B2B 以伺服器 、 IPC 工控及邊緣 AI 為主要拓展 ， 提供記憶體與儲存方案整合 、 客製韌體 、 技術支援及可靠度驗證 。
隨著 AI 推論由大型資料中心向邊緣端擴散，不同終端設備對溫度、功耗、可靠度需求多元，具備技術支援及客製
化能⼒的模組廠，有助提高客戶黏著度與產品附加價值。另在 B2C 業務方⾯，公司持續深耕 T-FORCE 電競及 T-
CREATE 創作者市場，同時，過去十年在消費性產品中，高階電競與創作者模組占比已超過七成；在消費者對高效
能、大容量及超頻產品仍有需求的情況下，有助降低低階 PC 需求疲弱衝擊。整體而言，2026 年營運受惠記憶體
價格上漲及 B2B 比重提高，2027 年隨著新廠投產、企業級客戶導入及邊緣 AI 需求；若能維持穩定貨源並有效控
制庫存成本，獲利有望維持高檔。預估 2027 年營收 436.8 億元，毛利率 46.1%，稅後 EPS 169.64 元，目標價
340 元(2 倍 PER*2027EPS(F))。

2026.11.02   
기아 (000270)

26.3Q Review: 수익성에 대한 입증이 필요  Company Brief

26.3Q Review: 매출액 31.6조원, 영업이익 1.8조원  
기아는 26.3Q 매출액 31.6조원 (+10.1% YoY, -4.4% QoQ), 영업이익 1.8조원 
(+24.3% YoY, -30.8% QoQ, +5.8% OPM) 등의 실적을 기록했다. 26.3Q 영업이익은 
시장 컨센서스 (2.36조원)를 22.9% 하회했다 [iM증권 추정치: 매출액 31.5조원, 
영업이익 2.3조원]. 주요 구성 요소별 내용은 아래와 같다. 
① P: 26.3Q 26.3Q 전동화 차량 판매는 30.0만대 (+47% YoY, 판매 비중 
36.2%)를 기록했는데, 이 중 HEV 17.2 만대 (+46% YoY), EV 12.3 만대 
(+75% YoY)로 구성됨. 미국 HEV 판매가 전년 동기 대비 118% 증가하며 
시장 성장률을 약 4배 상회했고, 특히 서유럽 EV 판매가 전년 대비 163% 
증가함. 단 26.3Q 중 원화가 빠르게 강세로 전환되며 원/달러, 원/유로 
평균 환율은 전분기 대비 각 5.2%, 6.2% 하락함에 따른 – 효과 관찰됨. 
② Q: 26.3Q 도매 판매는 84.7만대 (+7.9% YoY) 를 기록했고, 북미, 유럽, 
인도 등 주요 시장에서 고른 성장 나타남. 26.3Q 소매 판매 또한 82.9만대 
(+6.9% YoY)로 글로벌 산업 수요 (-6% YoY)를 상회. 북미는 HEV, 내수와 
유럽은 EV, 인도는 신형 셀토스와 소넷을 중심으로 판매가 확대됨 (이하 
26.3Q 권역별 도매 판매 YoY, 국내: +2.2%, 북미: +7.6%. 유럽: +19.3%. 
인도: +38.5%). 이란 사태 장기화로 아중동 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 
28.4% 감소했으나, 수요가 견조한 중남미와 CIS로 물량을 전환하며 
감소분을 상당 부분 만회함. 연간 도매 판매 가이던스 (335만대)를 유지함. 
③ C: 26.3Q 관세 비용은 약 0.7조원으로 추정되며, 전년 동기 대비 
5,000억원 감소함 (25.2Q 0.7조원, 25.3Q 1.2조원, 25.4Q 1.0조원, 26.1Q 
0.8조원). 반면 인센티브 증가 및 가격 인하 7,270억원, 서유럽 EV 비중 
확대에 따른 믹스 악화 4,650억원, 원자재 가격 상승 영향 3,680억원 등 
총 1.6조원의 감익 요인이 발생함. 이에 26.3Q 매출원가율은 84.1%로 
전년 동기 대비 3.0%p 상승함 (인센티브 +1.9%p, 재료비 +1.2%p). 다만 
26.3Q 기말 환율 급락하며 판매보증충당부채 환입 규모가 축소되었고 
판매보증비 약 9,000억원 감소. 판매관리비율은 10.1%로 3.7%p 개선됨. 
26.3Q 기아 순이익은 1.29조원으로 전년 동기 대비 9.6% 감소했다. 원화 강세에 
따라 외환환산 관련 이익이 전년 동기 540억원 이익에서 3,718억원으로 손실 
전환됐고, 현대모비스 등 관계사 이익 감소로 지분법손익 또한 360억원 감소했기 
때문이다 (현대차 순이익 감소 논리와 동일). 1) 산업 수요 감소와 중동 판매 차질에도 
권역별 파워트레인 대응을 통해 그룹사 대비 가지고 있는 외형 성장성을 증명했으나, 
2) 유럽 EV 판매 확대 과정에서 발생한 인센티브와 믹스 악화라는 비용 부담, 원자재 
가격 상승이 관세 감소 효과를 대부분 반납했고, 3) 분기 중 급격한 원화 강세가 
더해지며 시장 기대를 크게 하회하는 수익성을 기록했다는 것이 26.3Q 실적 총평이다.

실적 발표 후 주가 급락: 성장에도 불구하고 수익성에 대한 우려  
26.10.30 실적 발표 당일 기아 주가는 11.7% 하락했다. 하루 전 발표된 그룹사 실적 
발표와 상반된 모습이었다. 시장이 이번 실적에서 확인한 우려는 아래와 같다. 첫째, 
성장과 이익의 괴리다. 관세 영향을 제외한 26.3Q 영업이익은 2.52조원으로 전년 
동기(2.70조원) 대비 오히려 1,770억원 감소했다. 판매 증가에 따른 물량 효과 
(+4,420억원)와 환율 효과 (+5,290억원)가 발생했음에도, 인센티브와 믹스 악화로 이를 
대부분 반납한 것이다. 기아의 외형 성장 상당 부분이 유럽 EV 판매 증가에서 나오는데, 
해당 시장에서는 중국 EV 업체와의 경쟁으로 공격적인 프로모션이 불가피하다. 26.3Q 
유럽 권역에서의 중소형 EV 판매가 사업 계획을 크게 웃돌며 EV 믹스는 13.1%에서 
14.8%로 확대됐다. 시장은 이를 M/S를 위해 수익성을 훼손하고 있다고 받아들이는 
것이다. 둘째, 당장 26.4Q에도 유의미한 수익성 개선이 없을 것이라는 점이다. 기아는 
26.3Q 컨퍼런스 콜에서 4Q 영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이며, 연초 
제시한 연간 가이던스 (영업이익 10.2조원, +8.3% OPM) 에 다소 미달할 것이라고 
밝혔다. 중동 분쟁의 영향이 최소 연말까지 이어지고, 원자재와 물류비 부담 또한 
지속될 것이 회사의 판단이다. 무엇보다 원/달러 환율이 26.2Q 평균 약 1,505원에서 
26.3Q 약 1,430원, 현재 1,320~1,340원 수준까지 하락하며 이에 따른 - 영향이 커질 
수 있다. 셋째, 그룹사와의 이익 체력 비교다. 현대차는 26.3Q 파업과 생산 차질로 도매 
판매가 11.3% 감소했음에도 원가 절감을 통해 영업이익이 5.5% 증가했고, 시장 
기대를 상회했다. 반면 기아는 판매가 7.9% 증가했음에도 영업이익이 컨센서스를 20% 
이상 하회했다. 26.3Q 양사 영업이익 격차는 약 8,600억원까지 벌어진 상황이다.  
단기 노이즈를 제외한다면 기아의 기업 가치는 여전히 높다  
그럼에도 당사는 기아의 EV 전략, 낮은 수익성을 이유로 중장기 기업 가치에 대한 
기대를 낮출 이유는 없다고 판단한다. 첫째, 외부 요인을 걷어내면 본원적인 수익 
창출력은 유지되고 있다. 기아는 26.3Q 영업이익률을 낮춘 외부 요인을 원자재 1.2%p, 
중동 판매 차질 0.7%p, 환율 1.7%p 등 총 3.6%p로 분석했다. 즉 중동 분쟁이 
해소되면 상당 부분이 사라질 일회성 요인이다. 둘째, EV 수익성 개선 경로는 HEV가 
걸어온 길과 같다. 기아는 HEV 역시 도입 초기에는 수익을 내지 못했으나 현재는 ICE에 
버금가는 수익성을 내고 있다며, 26~27 년 현재의 EV 전략을 유지하겠다고 밝혔다. 
유럽 ICE 시장은 26.3Q 전년 동기 대비 약 20% 축소된 반면 EV 시장은 50% 이상 
성장하고 있다. 전환기에 스케일을 확보하지 못하면 EV 마진을 개선할 방법도 없다. 
기아는 향후 3년간 배터리, 모터, 히트펌프 등 주요 부품에서 연 10% 수준의 원가 
절감을 추진하고, 2~3년 내 EV를 HEV와 같은 수익성 경로에 올려놓겠다는 계획이다. 
셋째, 중장기 성장 계획을 수정하지 않았다. 기아는 27년 사업 계획이 CID 당시 제시한 
숫자와 큰 차이가 없을 것이며, 중장기 영업이익률 10% 목표도 유지했다. IAA 시행에 
대비해 슬로바키아 공장에서 EV를 전량 생산하고 ICE 국내 수출 방안도 검토 중이다. 
주주환원 측면에서도 TSR 35% 를 지키고, 자사주 매입보다 배당을 우선하겠다는 
입장이다. 10/30 종가와 최소 DPS 기준만으로도 배당수익률은 6.3%에 달하고 있다.

기아 판매관리비 (좌) 판매관리비율 (우)
  26.4Q 기아 매출액 31.8조원, 영업이익 2.2조원 전망 
기아 26.4Q 실적을 매출액 31.8조원 (+13.2% YoY), 영업이익 2.2조원 (+20.8% YoY, 
+7.0% OPM)으로 전망한다. 26.4Q 판매 성장세는 이어지고, 3Q에 집중 반영된 원자재 
부담 일부 완화와 계절적 성수기 효과로 3Q 대비 수익성은 소폭 개선될 것이다. 다만 
1) 원화 강세에 따른 평균 환율 하락, 2) 유럽 EV 인센티브의 높은 수준 유지 (미국은 
할부 금리 상승으로 QoQ 약 200달러 증가, 유럽은 전분기와 유사), 3) 중동 판매 
차질과 원자재, 물류비 부담 지속을 감안하면 의미 있는 반등은 어렵다. 이는 4Q 
영업이익률이 3Q 수준과 크게 다르지 않을 것이라는 회사의 언급과도 궤를 같이한다. 
기아 목표주가 150,000원으로 하향  
기아 목표주가를 26.4Q 추정치 하향을 반영해 150,000원으로 하향한다. 기아의 판매 
경쟁력은 금번 분기에도 증명됐다. 다만 EV 판매 확대가 수익성 개선으로 연결된다는 
점이 숫자로 확인되기 전까지, 시장은 기아의 성장에 할인율을 적용할 것이다. 중동 
분쟁 해소와 EV 원가 절감이 맞물리는 27년이 기아가 수익성을 증명할 시점이 될 
것이며, 6%를 상회하는 배당수익률이 그때까지 주가의 하방을 지지할 것으로 판단된다.

 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상

주가 추이

3분기 영업 이 익 은 지상방산 매출 감소와 일 회성 비용 등의 영 향 으로 
기대치를 대폭 하회했습니다. 4분기 실적 회복 과 중 장기 실적 성장 대
비 저평가된 밸 류 에이 션 에 주목합 니 다. 
3Q26 Review: 영업이익 0.5조원으로 컨센서스 대폭 하회 
3분기 매출액은 6.1조원으로 전년대비 6.0% 감소했다. 항공우주 부문
과 연결자회사들 성장에도 불구하고 지상방산 매출이 전년대비 약 0.9
조원 감소한 영향이다. 지상방산 내수와 수출 매출은 각각 0.7조원, 0.6
조원으로 전년대비 28.6%, 50.4% 감소했다. 내수는 대전 사업장 생산 
중단, 수출은 4분기 납기 집중에 기인한다. 항공우주 매출은 군수사업 
물량 증가로 전년대비 9.3% 증가한 0.7조원을 기록했다. 연결 자회사
인 한화오션과 한화시스템 매출은 각각 3.4조원, 0.8조원으로 전년대비 
12.9%, 30.1% 증가했다. 영업이익은 0.5조원으로 전년대비 49.2% 감
소했으며 영업이익률은 7.4%로 6.3%P 하락했다. 한화오션과 한화시스
템의 이익 증가에도 지상방산 이익 감소와 필리조선소 적자 반영이 영
향을 미쳤다. 지상방산은 일회성 비용 약 460억원, 원화 강세, 매출 감
소에 따른 고정비 부담 증가가 복합적으로 작용했다. 세전이익은 한화
오션의 외화환산손실 영향으로 -0.2조원을 기록하며 적자전환했다.  
실적 회복과 저평가 매력에 주목할 시점 
4분기 영업이익은 1.7조원으로 추정하며 시장 기대치를 상회할 전망이
다. 지상방산 수출 인도 물량이 집중되는 영향이다. 2027년 예상 실적 
기준 P/E는 12.5배로 경쟁사 Rheinmetall(22.5배) 대비 낮다. 약 35조
원의 방산 수주잔고를 기반으로 최소 2029년까지 실적 성장이 예상되
는 만큼 저평가 매력이 높다. 신규 수주 시점과 실적에 대한 우려가 상
존하지만 현재 밸류에이션에는 상당 부분 반영된 것으로 판단한다. 미
국 중간선거 등 국방예산 관련 정책 불확실성과 환율 부담이 완화되면 
투자자들의 관심은 4분기 실적과 2027년 신규 수주로 이동할 전망이
다. 연말 주요국의 국방예산 확정은 신규 수주 시점에 대한 불확실성을 
낮추는 계기가 될 수 있다.  
투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 1,300,000원 유지 
글로벌 재무장 수요 확대에 따른 동사의 중장기 성장 전망은 여전히 
유효하다. 투자의견 BUY와 목표주가 1,300,000원을 유지한다.

반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (채운샘) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
한화에어로스페이스는 당사와 ‘독점규제 및 공정거래에 관한 법률’ 에 따른 계열회사 관계에 있는 법인입니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

[ 한화에어로스페이스 주가 및 목표주가 추이 ]

한화에어로스페이스 (012450) [한화리서치]

6 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

## Document 265: AI安全测试引发黑客事件，以色列公司承认系人为失误所致

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI安全测试引发黑客事件，以色列公司承认系人为失误所致.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

以⾊列⽹络安全公司 Irregular Security ⾸席执⾏官 Dan Lahav 公开承认，近期涉及
Anthropic 、 OpenAI 、 Meta 及⾕歌四⼤ AI 巨头的⽹络攻击事件，根源在于公司内部测试配
置失误 —— 安全隔离控制未正确设置，导致 AI 模型突破沙盒环境并⼊侵外部系统。 Lahav
强调，这属于流程管理失误。
摘要：
以⾊列⽹络安全公司 Irregular Security ⾸席执⾏官承认，近期涉及多家顶级 AI 模型开发商的⼀系
列⽹络攻击事件，根源在于该公司内部的⼈为失误。
11 ⽉ 3 ⽇周⼆，据彭博报道， Irregular Security ⾸席执⾏官 Dan Lahav 在伦敦彭博科技峰会上表
⽰，测试期间应有的安全隔离控制未被正确配置，导致 AI 模型突破封闭的沙盒测试环境，接⼊外
部互联⽹，并在部分案例中对外部⽹站及计算机系统实施了可能违法的⼊侵⾏为。
Lahav 此番公开表态，是迄今最直接的说明，但部分⽹络安全专家此前已指出，若安全护栏配置

得当，这些事件本可避免。
Anthropic 、 OpenAI 、 Meta 及⾕歌均已披露了相关事件。
这⼀系列事件在全球范围内引发对 AI 发展速度的⼴泛警惕，并加剧了外界对前沿模型是否已超出
⼈类有效管控的担忧。
配置失误是根本原因，⾮模型主动绕过安全机制
Dan Lahav 在演讲中明确区分了两种不同性质的失败：是测试配置本⾝出错，⽽⾮ AI 模型主动颠
覆安全机制。 Dan Lahav 说：
这⼀定性对于理解事件性质⾄关重要。若属于模型主动突破安全边界，将意味着 AI 系统已具备
对抗⼈类控制的能⼒，其影响将远⽐操作失误更为严峻。Lahav 的说法将事件归咎于流程管理层
⾯。
然⽽， Lahav ⾄今披露的技术细节仍然有限。今年 8 ⽉，他曾发表⼀篇逾 1.1 万字的博⽂，但该⽂
并未正⾯回应具体技术问题，⽽是着重探讨其所称的 "AI 安全⾛向的紧迫议题 " 。
四⼤ AI 巨头均涉其中，安全测试机制受审视
据彭博报道， Anthropic 、 OpenAI 、 Meta 及⾕歌在过去六个⽉内相继披露了⿊客事件，这些事件

均发⽣于 Irregular Security 主导的模型安全评估测试期间。
AI 模型评估旨在帮助开发商在产品公开发布前识别潜在⻛险并建⽴相应防护措施。
Irregular Security 采取的评估模式是深度嵌⼊前沿 AI 实验室内部，对模型进⾏测试。这种模式赋
予该公司⾼度的内部访问权限，使其站在了有关 AI 技术负责任部署这⼀核⼼议题的前沿位置。
由于⼀个失误导致误解，隔离防护措施未能按照特定测试的预期设置。

## Document 292: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: Merrill Lynch (CMO)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The U.S. Capex Boom No One is Talking About—And Its Implications
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
We have long been of the belief that the U.S. is on the cusp of an inbound FDI capex boom
that can result in numerous positive spillover effects for the U.S. economy. The latest

figures from the U.S. Department of Commerce bear this out.
Exhibit 1 highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be inside the U.S.,
with FDI totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year. That is a staggering
103% jump from a year ago and places FDI inflows on track for a record year.
The latest inbound capex already adds to the massive scale of foreign investment in the
U.S. To wit, the accumulated stock of FDI in the U.S. stands at nearly $6T, based on the

latest figures from the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA). Manufacturing accounts for
roughly 43% of the total, and, at last count, some 10,000 foreign affiliates were operating
in the U.S.
These firms are largely invisible to the average U.S. investor, but the activities of U.S.
affiliates of foreign multinationals are substantial and competitive- and growth-enhancing.
To this point and based on the latest data from the BEA, foreign affiliates in the U.S.

employed nearly 8.6 million U.S. workers in 2024. That represents 6.1% of total private
sector employment, with manufacturing and retail the two most prominent sectors
of employment. Roughly 24% of all manufacturing jobs in the U.S. are from foreign
subsidiaries. In terms of pay, U.S. workers at these firms make $89,000 annually, 7% higher
than the national average, according to the Global Business Alliance.
Foreign affiliates also contributed some $1.5T to U.S. GDP; comprised some 15.7% of total

U.S. private sector investment; accounted for 12.4% of total U.S. research & development
(R&D) expenditures; paid roughly 20% of all U.S. federal taxes; and made up 22.5% of
U.S. exports and 26.2% of U.S. imports. 1 These are the direct effects of U.S. subsidiaries
of foreign companies operating in the U.S. The indirect effects are even greater in terms
of more income, jobs, growth and tax revenue—all bolstering the long-term competitive
advantage of the U.S. In a nutshell, foreign firms operating in the U.S. are an important

ingredient of U.S. economic growth.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Companies that once served the U.S. market exclusively through exports
increasingly face higher costs and more uncertainties and hence want to be “inside.”
Two, with a $32T-plus economy with extraordinary consumer purchasing power, foreign

firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment than just trade (exports). There
is no more dynamic consumer in the world than the American shopper.
Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers, semiconductors, power
generation, etc., has created numerous investment opportunities for many foreign firms
that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging capex cycle.
Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly developed

technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.
As more U.S. affiliates of foreign multinationals set up shop in the U.S., think more job
creation across the country, more manufacturing output, more locally sourced purchases,
more U.S. exports, more spending on R&D, and more tax revenue for local communities.
And over the long run, think a more globally competitive U.S. economy.
1 Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign Multinational Enterprises, 2024” and “Activities of U.S. Affiliates of Foreign

Multinational Enterprises, 2023”, BEA July 2026.
Investment Implications
The boom in foreign investment in
the U.S. should continue to support
employment, productivity, corporate
earnings, and broader U.S. economic
competitiveness, in our view. Hence
our U.S. bias.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
And speaking of global competitiveness, Exhibit 2A is very illustrative of the magnet pull of
the U.S. economy to attract FDI versus the rest of the world. Of new greenfield investment
this decade, note the overriding preference for the U.S., accounting for nearly 16% of total
global greenfield investment this decade. America’s share is larger than the next three
countries combined—the United Kingdom, India, and Germany. And note that America’s

share of new investment is more than 5X that of China’s. Most of this new investment
has gone into manufacturing sectors like electrical equipment, computers, appliances and
batteries and has originated from Asia and Europe.
The bulk of foreign investment comes from Europe, followed by Japan and Canada, but as
Exhibit 2B highlights, there is a new dynamic playing out when it comes to America’s ability
to attract capital. There is a new source in town: a handful of developing nations (namely

Taiwan, South Korea, Mexico and Brazil). Mexican manufacturers, Taiwan technology
leaders, South Korea chip leaders, Brazil industrial firms, India pharmaceutical firms—these
companies and more are realizing that in today’s trade environment, the best way to serve
the American market is not necessarily through exports but through investment—investing
locally. FDI flows from developing nations reached $63B in the 12 months through June,
more than double the level a year prior ($24.5B), according to the BEA. Fueled by insatiable

AI demand, flows from South Korea alone totaled $23.4B in the first half of 2026. The
upshot is that an increasingly important source of capital for the U.S. is coming from an
unlikely source: the developing nations.
More fire for the capex boom. The coming boom in FDI inflows is supportive of our thesis
that the U.S. stands on the cusp of a major industrial upgrade. We are in the early innings
of a structural—not cyclical—capex cycle that should lead to the rebuilding of domestic

supply chains, higher value-added manufacturing, and more AI-driven productivity growth.
Foreign firms, in our opinion, will be part of this process.
That is important to recognize because remember the following: when a foreign company
builds a factory or an R&D facility or expands its existing manufacturing capacity right
here in the U.S., good things happen. Demand for U.S. goods and services goes up (as does
demand for construction, materials, logistics and professional services). New jobs are created,

boosting incomes of U.S. workers, while supporting more local spending and consumption.
And because many foreign firms often bring advanced manufacturing technologies when
investing in the U.S., the end results include a boost in productivity and innovation in various
sectors of the economy.
All the above, of course, are the secret ingredients to stronger U.S. economic growth, rising
corporate earnings and higher equity prices. With a little help from our friends, America’s

industrial renewal is gaining strength and momentum.

4 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Europe’s Electoral Cycle and Market Risks
Ehiwario Efeyini, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
As midterm elections approach for the U.S. next month, voters across Europe will also
enter their own election season starting this November and continuing throughout
2027. Over the next year, 52% of the total EU population, representing 46% of EU GDP
across eight countries, will go to the polls, including voters in three of its four largest

economies (Exhibit 3). Having announced snap general elections earlier this month,
Spain will vote for members of its lower and upper house on November 29. A series of
parliamentary elections are then due for 2027 in Estonia (by March 7), Finland (on April
18), Greece (by July 25), Slovakia (by September 28), Poland (by November 11) and Italy
(by December 22). And perhaps most consequentially France will stage Presidential
elections on April 18, with a potential second round of voting set for May 2.

For the EU as a whole, the significance of these coming elections will be the potential
for a shift toward more populist, domestically oriented policy priorities from leading
opposition candidates (particularly in France, Spain and Poland) which could obstruct the
process of closer EU integration. In the nearer term, adoption of the new seven-year
(2028-2034) EU budget due by the end of 2027 could be impeded. And over the longer
term, renewed efforts toward an EU-wide capital market union to deepen and attract

more inward investment into European markets could also be further delayed.
At the country level, investors have seen bond yields rise globally in 2026 on the back of
higher inflation and concerns over fiscal sustainability. The debt question has arguably
been most pressing in parts of Europe, especially given the likelihood that upcoming
elections make for domestic increases in government largesse. And of the eight
countries going to the polls over the next year, France remains the biggest source of

concern. Interest rate spreads on French 10-year sovereign bonds have overtaken those
in both Greece and Italy this year (Exhibit 4), driven by investor concerns over public
debt. The 2027 government budget unveiled earlier this month projected a deficit of
5.0% of GDP (well in excess of the 3% specified in the EU Stability and Growth Pact),
but remained insufficiently expansive to nullify the risk that the current government
could be unseated later this year if it is forced through the fragmented parliament. This

comes on top of an existing debt load of close to 120% of GDP and a halving of official
2026 growth forecasts from 1.0% at the start of the year to 0.5% in September. In
short, all four variables in France’s debt sustainability equation currently stand as major
headwinds, and the two leading candidates in the 2027 election have shown limited
appetite for fiscal consolidation over the coming years.
Exhibit 3: Europe's Upcoming Elections Represent Around Half of EU GDP and Population.

Investment Implications
We remain cautious on European
markets overall, despite seeing
pockets of strength in selected
areas such as defense, data center
infrastructure and renewable energy.
On top of growing uncertainty
from the upcoming electoral cycle

and interest rate outlook, rising
competition and slowing demand
from China continue to challenge
key segments of the Consumer
Discretionary sector. And limited
direct AI concentration also leaves
Europe under-exposed to the key
growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and
emerging Asia.

Intervention from the ECB under its 2022 bond purchase program is also unlikely at this

stage given that the spread widening in France has not appeared either disorderly or
unwarranted. Meanwhile, conditions could potentially be aggravated by expected ECB rate
hikes in response to still above-target inflation across the eurozone. Indeed recent moves
to tighten monetary policy from a number of major central banks have been read most
hawkishly in Europe. Most of the increase in European bond yields has come from higher
inflation breakevens as opposed to real yields in the U.S. and Japan. And despite some

paring back of tightening expectations in the wake of recent bond volatility, the market is
still pricing three more policy rate increases over the next 12 months.
Euro depreciation and an emerging shift toward more protectionist trade policy with China
could further complicate the ECB policy outlook. China posted a record $35B trade surplus,
according to Eurostat, with the EU in September (alongside its $938B total surplus for the
first three quarters of 2026), and the two markets have agreed to reduce the EU bilateral

deficit by capping imports of hybrid cars for four years. Hybrids account for less than 2%
of total EU imports from China, but this could potentially mark the start of a broader shift
toward more trade protection in Europe with its largest foreign supplier which risks adding
further upward pressure to cost-push inflation. And though the leading candidates in the
French election may favor resuming oil and gas imports from Russia, Europe also remains

among the most at risk from the global energy shock. Indeed some 56% of the region’s
crude oil consumption is in the form of diesel product according to the International Energy
Agency, more than twice the U.S. share.
The 2- to 10-year eurozone yield curve has already flattened significantly since the start
of the year. And at just over 30 basis points as of mid-October, it remains flatter than
its equivalents in the U.S., UK and Japan. Outside Energy and Technology, Financials have

been among the strongest-performing sectors in Europe this year. But additional ECB
rate hikes could lead to further curve flattening, compressing bank net interest margins
and contributing to slower growth—particularly given that rate increases are coming in
response to supply-driven inflation rather than reflecting stronger economic activity.
We nonetheless continue to see pockets of strength in European markets. Growing
defense budgets should remain a tailwind for Industrials as national governments look to

meet their North Atlantic Treaty Organization targets over the next several years. And the
sector should be further supported by indirect exposure to AI investment through electric
cabling, power infrastructure and building materials for data centers. Leaders in renewable
energy equipment and services are also likely to be beneficiaries of grid upgrades and
broadening electrification globally, particularly given elevated fossil fuel prices. But our view

on the region remains cautious overall. On top of growing uncertainty from the upcoming
electoral cycle and interest rate outlook, rising competition and slowing demand from
China continue to challenge key segments of Europe’s Consumer Discretionary sector.
Limited direct AI concentration in technology hardware, cloud services and application
software also leaves Europe underexposed to the key growth theme that continues to buoy
equity markets in the U.S., Japan and emerging Asia.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 19, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Cautious Optimism Entering Fiscal Year 2027
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
“A renewed focus on rebuilding fiscal space is urgent, given elevated debt, higher
borrowing costs, multiplying demands on public budgets and heightened uncertainty.” So
warned the IMF about the global fiscal outlook last week in its updated World Economic

Outlook report. Much is at stake for developed nations in Europe and Asia facing high
existing debt, unfavorable demographics and more modest growth.
For the U.S., between structurally higher mandatory outlays, the administration’s $1.5T
proposed defense package, and the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield sitting above 5%, spending
is likely headed higher. We continue to fade concerns of an impending U.S. debt crisis,
however, largely because economic growth continues to surprise to the upside. Expanding

output is good news for the debt-to-GDP ratio, which remains a touch below 100%.
Indeed, rather than being led higher on account of un-anchored inflation expectations, the
10-year U.S. Treasury yield has repriced to reflect better-than-expected nominal growth
(Exhibit 5A). Higher yields in the face of slowing growth, meanwhile, would be a different
story, not only for the fiscal outlook (worse) but also for U.S. equity valuations (lower).
Of course, after the U.S. accumulated another $2T deficit in FY 2026, 2 “rebuilding fiscal

space” in the IMF’s words will be politically unpopular and mathematically challenging.
In our view, the biggest near-term tail risk would be a surprise move lower in growth as
budget deficits at 6% of GDP in good times leave little room for bad times. This isn’t our
base case given robust consumer spending per our internal credit and debit card data and
ongoing investment in the AI buildout and beyond. We expect these two pillars to sustain
growth in 2027.

The other good news is that, contrary to concerns, demand for Treasurys remains robust.
Yields are attractive—that was the message from October’s successful $39B 10-year
Treasury note auction. At the same time, U.S. debt boasts an array of buyers: domestic
households, the Federal Reserve, mutual funds and exchange-traded funds, money market
funds, pension funds, foreign central banks, private investors overseas, and more. Per
Exhibit 5B, whereas foreign official investors once dominated some 40% of the U.S.

Treasury market during the 2008/2009 Great Financial Crisis, their share has fallen to
12% while domestic private investors now account for a majority of holdings. Translated:
demand is more diverse than most appreciate.

for investors seeking income and can

the U.S. over developed economies in

fiscal instability.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 315: Downgrade of global equities to a Core allocation

### Page 1

Downgrade of global 
equities to a Core allocation  
Summary 
Event: We are downgrading global equities from Overweight 
to a Neutral/Core allocation. We continue to maintain a 
Neutral allocation to fixed income, Overweight on Gold and 
Underweight on Cash. There are more details in our Global 
Market Outlook publication on 30 October 2026. 
What does the equity downgrade mean?  
This downgrade implies a slightly lower allocation to global 
equities within a diversified portfolio. For our CIO Foundation 
Balanced portfolio, this would imply an equity allocation of 
52.5%, down from 53 .2%. We continue to expect positive 
returns for global equities  in the coming 6 -12 months , 
though equity investors may need to recalibrate expectations 
lower, compared with the 15 -20% annualised gains 
experienced in the last few years. Our capital market 
assumptions still suggest a mid -to-high single digit annual 
returns over the next few years, consistent with a transition 
to a mid -cycle bull market . Consensus estimates point to 
16% earnings growth for global equities in 2027, which 
should overcome various headwinds to deliver positive 
returns for equities over the next 12 months. Investors should 
thus, continue to maintain exposure to equities  within a 
diversified portfolio. 
In addition, we see a changing playbook  to prepare for 
higher volatility, more active management and greater 
diversification, as discussed below. 
What does it mean for regional equities 
positioning? 
Within a global equities portfolio, we remain Overweight US 
and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) equities , which are expected to 
deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 amongst the 
major regions. This growth is led by the technology sector, 
which carries the largest weight in both regions. Within the 
diversified equities portfolio, we maintain Core allocations to

Euro area equities, which offer lower valuations, and Japan 
equities, which is backed by a reflating economy. We are 
Underweight UK equities, where the sector composition 
offers lower growth prospects in 2027. Within the AxJ region, 
we are Overweight Taiwan equities, with attractive structural 
growth driven by its semiconductor industry. We are also 
Overweight India equities, which offers diversified non-tech 
growth drivers. Taiwan and India equities both generate 
attractive returns on equity. We maintain Core allocations to 
China equities, with low valuations, and Korea equities, with 
explosive earnings growth in its semiconductors industry, but 
volatile price movements. We are Underweight ASEAN, 
which offers relatively lower earnings growth. 
What are the implications for equity sectors? 
We are Overweight these sectors globally: US 
information technology, US healthcare, US financials, US 
materials, Europe ex -UK financials, and Japan 
financials. 
A framework we use is to break the market into four key 
sector building blocks. Tech is the largest block, 
encompassing the Information Technology, Communication 
Services and Consumer Discretionary sectors. Put 
differently, the segment spans five major industries: 
semiconductors, hardware, internet, software, and IT 
services. Within the US market, we expect this segment to 
deliver c.30% earnings growth in 2027, extending the strong 
momentum seen in 2026. 
Financials and Real Estate  are the next building block, 
comprising these two sectors. We expect this segment to 
continue delivering low -teens earnings growth in 2027, 
supported by resilient economic activity and healthy credit 
trends. 
Resources include companies across four sectors: 
Industrials, Materials, Energy, and Utilities . We expect the 
segment to generate 10 -15% earnings growth in 2027, 
although Energy is likely to remain the largest drag on overall 
growth due to tougher comparisons and softer energy price 
assumptions for 2027. 
Healthcare & Others  consists primarily of Healthcare and 
Consumer Staples. We expect mid-teen earnings growth for 
the segment in 2027, with a rebound in healthcare earnings 
representing a potential upside surprise. 
Our Overweight sector exposures are drawn from each of the 
four key blocks above. Unlike the last few years , with 
concentrated gains from Tech providing market leadership, 
we see our downgrade in global equities also reflecting a 
broadening in market leadership towards each of the other 
sector blocks apart from Tech.

As structural themes such as AI transition from the rapid 
buildout phase towards monetisation, diversified exposure 
across sectors and regions should become increasingly 
important. 
What are the implications for equity 
opportunistic ideas? 
We have opportunistic ideas on Global High -Dividend 
equities, US healthcare, Euro area banks , MSCI 
Switzerland and Japan banks. The selection represents a 
more defensive stance  currently, with an average beta of 
below 1 relative to global equities (MSCI AC World), but 
attractive drivers nonetheless, for each of the ideas.  
Global High-Dividend offers higher-income equity allocation 
with historically lower volatility than global equities. US 
healthcare is supported by wider adoption of obesity 
treatments, advance s in oncology a nd innovative medical 
devices, that support earnings growth. Euro area banks  
continue to see steady earnings growth adding to their 
excess capital, which is being returned to shareholders via 
attractive dividend and share buyback yields. Japan banks 
continue to enjoy net margin expansion from BoJ policy 
normalisation, as well as corporate governance 
improvements. MSCI Switzerland is a newly initiated idea for 
investors seeking defensive exposure. H ealthcare and 
consumer staples account s for more than half the index 
weight, providing exposure to relatively resilient demand that 
is more insulated from economic cycles and AI trends. It also 
has a sizeable financial sector supported by steady wealth 
management growth and well-capitalised insurers with 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We see the case for more active management  in a mid -
cycle transition, as higher market dispersion should create 
greater opportunities for active sector and stock selection, 
particularly as leadership within equities evolves. 
What are the implications of higher equity 
market volatility?  
A more uncertain political backdrop and p otentially maturing 
growth rates in certain market segments may contribute to 
more frequent bouts of market volatility. Higher volatility 
presents an opportunity to use structured strategies to gain 
exposure to equities. Such strategies may be used to 
generate income or to gain exposure at a discounted entry 
price. Investors can also consider strategies that are 
structured to return principal or partial principal at maturity. 
What alternative strategies are suited to a 
regime of moderating equity market returns?  
Investors can consider alternative strategies such as hedge  
fund strategies or private markets, for those with the capacity

to accept some illiquidity.  
Equity long/short hedge fund  strategies would retain growth 
participation with less market exposure. Fund managers buy 
companies they expect to outperform and short those they 
expect to underperform, seeking to earn the difference 
between the two , and benefit even as total equity market 
returns are moderating. 
Within private markets , private equity provides  a means to 
higher returns with the effects of an illiquidity premium. 
Meanwhile, i nfrastructure offers  differentiated exposure to 
structural growth opportunities, where c ontracted long-term 
revenues reduce dependence on equity-market sentiment. 
What are the implications on fixed income 
and FX markets? 
Bonds: This year’s surge in bond yields means bonds now 
offer a hedge against any equity market weakness. We 
believe the 3 -7-year part of the US bond yield curve offers 
the best hedge against any equity sell -off caused by AI -
related growth worries. This part of the bond curve has priced 
in most Fed rate hike expectations, which could reverse if 
growth concerns return. Meanwhile, we favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a hedge against any 
fuel price-led inflation scare.  
Credit: For corporate bonds, adopting a defensive stance in 
a volatile environment typically involves: i) increasing credit 
quality, favouring higher-rated issuers; and ii) moving up the 
capital structure, by reducing exposure to subordinated debt 
and favouring secured debt. From a sector perspective, 
large, diversified banks, utilities, and quasi -sovereigns tend 
to be more defensive, particularly relative to cyclical sectors, 
such as metals and mining, exploration, and technology.         
FX: We favour bearish AUD/JPY as a tactical hedge against 
any equity market correction. Review of 21 past S&P500 
correction sessions (2010 -2025) shows AUD weakened 
against USD in 86% of cases, while JPY strengthened 
against USD in 67%, supporting our bearish AUD/JPY 
outlook during any equity market stress.

## Document 421: Transitioning to mid-cycle (for Private Bank only)

Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds . Near-
term event risks and high  earnings expectations prompt us to trim  equities to a 
Core holding.  The US and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive  income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain  Overweight gold . Prices are likely to grind higher as near -term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high  earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead.  In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran . These  concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly f or the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite t he current c hallenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop . The US economic outlook remains well supported , and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long -term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects  this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar  by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and A xJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect the m to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers.  Our positive view on both markets’  
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India.  Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI -related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification . In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic idea s also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months  – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment -grade (IG) 
and high -yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3 -7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance.  We close our EU  bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 29 ) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective  tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream , while  rotating into alternatives  
helps reduce  equity beta exposure . We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub -
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4 ,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

The Foundation and Foundation+ models are allocations that you can use as the starting point for building a diversified 
investment portfolio. The Foundation model showcases a set of allocations focusing on traditional asset classes that are 
accessible to most investors, while the Found ation+ model includes allocations to private assets that may be accessible to 
investors in some jurisdictions, but not others.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary

Artificial intelligence (AI) has become one of the most powerful drivers of global market performance and, increasingly, 
one of the largest contributors to portfolio risk.  While we remain constructive on the long -term outlook for equities and 
continue to view AI as a transformational investment theme, our tactical positioning has recently shifted from an Overweight to 
a Neutral equity weight. This reflects a more balanced near -term risk -reward profile after a period of strong equity market 
performance and increasingly concentrated market leadership. 
Against this backdrop, a deeper understanding of portfolio construction is essential. Investors should consider not only 
how much they allocate to equities, bonds and alternative investments, but also the underlying factors driving portfolio risk. Using 
a Total Portfolio Approach (TPA), we assessed the factor exposures embedded within our Foundation and Foundation+ 
Balanced portfolios, with a particular focus on AI concentration risk. 
Our analysis suggests both portfolios are more diversified than a traditional 60/40 allocation  and maintain meaningful 
exposure to long -term AI growth opportunities.  The Foundation+ Balanced portfolio provides additional diversification 
through alternative investments and a broader range of return drivers, reducing reliance on public equity markets.

Key findings

Looking beyond asset classes: Understanding 
AI concentration risk 
Investors often assess diversification through the lens of asset 
allocation. A portfolio containing equities, bonds, gold and 
alternatives may appear diversified on the surface. However, 
this traditional approach alone does not always reveal where 
portfolio risks are truly concentrated. 
TPA starts from a different premise. Rather than focusing solely 
on asset classes, it seeks to identify the underlying factors that 
drive portfolio outcomes, including equity markets, interest 
rates, credit spreads, currencies, liquidity conditions and 
thematic exposures such as AI. 
This perspective is particularly relevant following our recent 
move from an Overweight to a Neutral equity allocation. While 
we remain constructive on equities over the long term, a 
growing share of market returns has been driven by a relatively 
narrow group of AI -related beneficiaries. For investors, t he 
question is, therefore, not whether to maintain exposure to AI, 
but whether their portfolios have become overly dependent on 
a single theme or underlying risk factor.

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8

US equities exhibit some of the highest levels of AI intensity, 
reflecting the dominant role US companies play across the AI 
value chain. By contrast, fixed income, private assets and gold 
display significantly lower AI sensitivity and derive their 
returns from different economic drivers. 
The objective is not to eliminate AI exposure. We continue to 
believe AI will remain one of the defining structural growth 
themes of the coming decade. Instead, the objective is to 
ensure portfolios participate in this opportunity without 
becoming excessively dependent on it. 
Stronger diversification through different 
return drivers 
One of the most common misconceptions is that greater 
diversification inevitably leads to lower returns. Our analysis 
suggests the opposite may be true when diversification is 
achieved through genuinely different return drivers. Both 
Foundation and Foundation + portfolios exhibit attractive risk-
return characteristics relative to a traditional 60/40 allocation 
(see Fig. 9). They deliver comparable or higher return 
potential with lower volatility, smaller drawdowns and reduced 
sensitivity to global equity markets.

The key insight is that diversification works best when risks 
originate from different economic drivers. Simply increasing 
the number of securities or asset classes is not sufficient. 
What matters is the breadth of return sources supporting 
portfolio outcomes. 
AI remains a meaningful component of portfolio risk , and its 
exposure is spread across several parts of the ecosystem.  
Non-AI-related factors , including equity beta, credit, 
currencies and liquidity premia, also drive portfolio risk . This 
broader mix reduces dependence on any single investment 
theme and creates a more balanced portfolio structure. 
The role of alternatives: Creating additional 
return engines 
The most important distinction between our Foundation and 
Foundation+ portfolios is the inclusion of hedge funds and 
private assets in the latter. Alternatives can provide additional 
return engines and reduce reliance on equity market beta, but 
different strategies offer very different forms of diversification. 
Within hedge funds, a multi-strategy provides a strong 
starting point for investors seeking lower equity sensitivity and 
differentiated return sources, with an equity beta of around 
0.15-0.20 and a correlation slightly above 50%  to global 
equities’ performance. Equity long/short retains greater 
participation in equity-led growth, reflected in a higher 0.45 
beta, and has historically captured 56% of global equity gains. 
However, it also maintains greater common exposure to 
equity and AI factors. Relative value offers the lowest 
equity exposure, with a beta of around 0.10 and participation 
below 5% of global equity losses , although nearly 80% of its 
modelled risk is attributed to credit (partly reflecting the use of 
convertible-arbitrage trades), highlighting that reducing equity 
beta can mean substituting it with another source of risk.  
Within private markets, the sources of return also differ 
materially. Private credit offers the most differentiated 
return engine, with returns driven primarily by income and 
credit premia rather than equity appreciation. Private equity, 
by contrast, remains more closely linked to equity and 
growth factors, making it a stronger source of long-term return 
potential but a less effective tool for reducing portfolio beta. 
Infrastructure can provide an attractive middle ground. It 
retains exposure to AI -related structural themes, particularly 
through digital and power infrastructure, and offers lower equity 
beta and correlation than traditional public equities. 
Ultimately, our move from an Overweight to a Core allocation 
in equity markets does not change our constructive long-term 
view on equities or AI. Rather, it reinforces the importance of 
understanding where portfolio risk originates. Investors do not 
need to choose between participating in the AI opportunity 
and maintaining diversific ation. Using a TPA lens combines 
exposure to long -term growth themes with a broader set of 
return drivers, including carefully selected alternatives,  and 
reduces dependence on any single source of market 
performance.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability):  We maintain the probability at 30% a s AI-driven investment growth  continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027 , as AI-led expansion continues.  The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4 %, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2. 3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026 , but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risk s 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures ( PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees c ore PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2. 4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates  at 4.0% , after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.
